\hat{C}_{\Omega}(x_{0}):=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{\eta}_{\Omega}(x_{0})\geq 1/2\}}
.
Our next two results concern the properties of
\hat{C}_{\Omega}(x_{0})
, and we begin by establishing the rate at which
\hat{\eta}_{\Omega}(x)
approximates
\eta(x)
on the event
E_{1}^{\delta}(x)\cap E_{2}^{\delta}(x)
. For
x\in\mathcal{X}
let
R^{\delta}_{\mathbf{0}_{d}}(x):=\sqrt{\frac{\log_{+}(2|\mathcal{N}|/\delta)}{2n}}
and for
\omega\in\mathcal{N}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
and
x\in\mathcal{X}
define

R^{\delta}_{\omega}(x):=\biggl(\frac{n_{\omega}^{-\frac{\alpha\beta_{\omega}^{2}/d_{\omega}}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}}}{\rho_{\omega}^{\beta_{\omega}/d_{\omega}}(x^{\omega})}+\log^{1/2}_{+}(2|\mathcal{N}|/\delta)\biggr)n_{\omega}^{-\frac{\beta_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}}.

This represents the rate at which
\hat{f}_{\omega}(x)
estimates
f_{\omega}(x)
on our high-probability event. Further, for
\Omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
satisfying
\Omega_{\star}\subseteq\Omega\cup L(\Omega)
and
x\in\mathcal{X}
, let

R^{\delta}_{\Omega}(x):=\max_{\omega\in\Omega}R^{\delta}_{\omega}(x).

Lemma
4
shows that
\hat{C}_{\Omega}
agrees with
C^{\mathrm{Bayes}}
on the event
E_{1}^{\delta}(x)\cap E_{2}^{\delta}(x)
, whenever
\eta
is not too close to
1/2
.

Lemma 4
.

Fix
d,n\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\{0,1\}^{d}
,
o_{1},\ldots,o_{n}\in\mathcal{O}
,
\Omega_{\star},\Omega\in\mathcal{I}(\{0,1\}^{d})\setminus\{\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\},\emptyset\}
,
c_{\mathrm{E}}\in(0,1)
,
\bm{\gamma}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{L}}\geq 1
,
\bm{\beta}\in(0,1]^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{S}}\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,\infty)
,
C_{\mathrm{M}}\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Suppose that
\Omega_{\star}\subseteq\Omega\cup L(\Omega)\subseteq\mathcal{N}
. Fix
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Miss}}
. Let

C:=\max_{\omega\in\Omega}\Bigl\{2\kappa_{\omega}|\Omega\cup L(\Omega)|\bigl(2^{3+d_{\omega}}C_{\mathrm{B}}C_{\mathrm{S}}+\sqrt{1/2}\bigr)\Bigr\}.

If
x\in\mathcal{X}
is such that

\displaystyle|\eta(x)-1/2|\geq C\cdot R_{\Omega}^{\delta}(x),

then, on the event
E_{1}^{\delta}(x)\cap E_{2}^{\delta}(x)
, we have
\hat{C}_{\Omega}(x)=C^{\mathrm{Bayes}}(x)
.

We are now in a position to state Proposition
5
.

Proposition 5
.

Fix
d,n\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\{0,1\}^{d}
,
o_{1},\ldots,o_{n}\in\mathcal{O}
,
\Omega_{\star},\Omega\in\mathcal{I}(\{0,1\}^{d})\setminus\{\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\},\emptyset\}
,
c_{\mathrm{E}}\in(0,1)
,
\bm{\gamma}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{L}}\geq 1
,
\bm{\beta}\in(0,1]^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{S}}\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,\infty)
,
C_{\mathrm{M}}\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Let
\delta^{\prime}:=\frac{\delta}{2}\max_{\omega\in\Omega}\{1/n_{\omega}^{1+\alpha}\}
. Suppose that
\Omega_{\star}\subseteq\Omega\cup L(\Omega)\subseteq\mathcal{N}
and
\min_{\omega\in\Omega}\gamma_{\omega}>\max_{\omega\in\Omega}\beta_{\omega}/d_{\omega}
.
There exists a constant
K_{\Omega}\geq 2
such that, for any
Q\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{\mathrm{Miss}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{Q}\biggl\{\mathcal{E}_{P}(\hat{C}_{\Omega})>K_{\Omega}\cdot\log_{+}^{\frac{1+\alpha}{2}}(2|\mathcal{N}|/\delta^{\prime})\cdot\max_{\omega\in\Omega}n_{\omega}^{-\frac{\beta_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}(1+\alpha)}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}}\biggm|O_{1}=o_{1},\ldots,O_{n}=o_{n}\biggr\}\!\leq\!\delta.

(19)

The idea behind the proof of this result is to integrate the bound on the regression function in Lemma
4
over
\mathcal{X}
. This involves ruling out an exceptional set on which the event
E_{1}^{\delta^{\prime}}(x)\cap E_{2}^{\delta^{\prime}}(x)
fails to hold; see Lemma
S28

in Section
S3.1

for full details. The proof of the upper bound in Theorem
1
now follows by bounding the expectation of the excess risk using (
19
), and replacing
\Omega
with
\Omega_{\star}
. The details are also deferred to Section
S3.1

.

5.3
Outline of the proof of Theorem
2

In this subsection we provide an outline for the proof of Theorem
2
. The full details of the proof are somewhat lengthy and are therefore given in Section
S3.2

in the supplementary material. The key idea is to show that our estimator
\hat{\Omega}
used in the HAM classifier in Algorithm
1
correctly identifies
\Omega_{\star}
with high probability; see Proposition
6
below. The proof of Theorem
2
then follows by carefully combining this proposition with the upper bound in Theorem
1
.

Before stating the proposition, we will use the constant
C_{\mathrm{B}}
(which provides an upper bound on
\|f_{\omega}\|_{\infty}
and only depends on
d
) from Proposition
S4

, and define
\phi_{\omega}:=\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}
,
\kappa_{\omega,\circ}:=2\phi_{\omega}/(\beta_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega})
and
C_{\rho}:=(32C^{2}_{\mathrm{B}}C_{\mathrm{L}})^{-\frac{1}{\min_{\omega\in\mathcal{N}}\gamma_{\omega}}}
. Further, we will make use of a constant
\kappa_{\omega,\mathrm{T}}
, which only depends on
d_{\omega}
; see the proof of Lemma
S34

in Section
S3.2

for a precise definition.

Proposition 6
.

Fix
d,\!n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\{0,\!1\}^{d}
,
o_{1},\ldots,o_{n}\in\mathcal{O}
,
\Omega_{\star}\in\mathcal{I}(\{0,1\}^{d})\setminus\{\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\},\emptyset\}
,
c_{\mathrm{E}}\in(0,1/4]
,
\bm{\gamma}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{L}}\geq 1
,
\bm{\beta}\in(0,1]^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{S}}\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,\infty)
,
C_{\mathrm{M}}\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,1)
. Suppose that
\Omega_{\star}\subseteq\mathcal{N}
and
\min_{\omega\in\Omega_{\star}}\gamma_{\omega}>\max_{\omega\in\Omega_{\star}}\beta_{\omega}/d_{\omega}
. Suppose further that
(n_{\omega})_{\omega\in\mathcal{N}}
satisfies

n_{\omega}\geq\Bigl(\frac{2^{7}C_{B}^{2}C_{\mathrm{L}}}{c_{\mathrm{E}}}\Bigr)^{\kappa_{\omega,\circ}(1\vee\frac{1}{2\alpha})}\Bigl(\frac{20C_{\mathrm{S}}\kappa_{\omega,\mathrm{T}}\kappa_{\omega,\circ}\cdot\log_{+}(2|\mathcal{N}|n_{\omega}/\delta)}{C_{\rho}^{1\vee\gamma_{\omega}}}\Bigr)^{2\kappa_{\omega,\circ}}

for all
\omega\in\Omega_{\star}
, and

n_{\omega}\geq\Bigl(16\kappa_{\omega,\circ}\kappa_{\omega,T}^{2}\cdot\log(2|\mathcal{N}|n_{\omega}/\delta)\Bigr)^{\kappa_{\omega,\circ}},

for all
\omega\in\mathcal{N}\cap U(\Omega_{\star})
.
Then we have

\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}^{+}}\mathbb{P}_{Q}\Bigl(\hat{\Omega}\neq\Omega_{\star}\Bigm|O_{1}=o_{1},\ldots,O_{n}=o_{n}\Bigr)\leq 4\delta.

Proposition
6
shows that, under a sample size condition, we have
\hat{\Omega}=\Omega_{\star}
with high probability, uniformly over the class
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}^{+}
. For the proof of this result, given in Section
S3.2

, we introduce several additional high-probability events, which control how well we can estimate
\sigma_{\omega}^{2}
as well as the error of
\hat{f}
at each of the training data points simultaneously. On the intersection of these events, we are then able to show that
\hat{\sigma}^{2}_{\omega}
is on the correct side of the threshold
\tau_{\omega}
for each relevant
\omega\in\mathcal{N}
. The sample size condition ensures that we have enough signal in our training data to detect that
f_{\omega}
is nonzero. Note, however, that in Theorem
2
we are in fact able to remove the condition on
n_{\omega}
for
\omega\in\Omega_{\star}
and simplify the condition for
\omega\in\mathcal{N}\cap U(\Omega_{\star})
.

The research of TS and TIC is supported by an Engineering and Physical Sciences Research Council (EPSRC) New Investigator Award (EP/V002694/1), and the research of TBB is supported by an EPSRC New Investigator Award (EP/W016117/1).

S1
Additional results and proofs of the claims in Section
2

S1.1
Intuition behind Definition
1

and proofs of the claim in the examples

Our first result below provides additional understanding of the class
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\mathcal{O})
in Definition
1

, based on conditional independence properties.

Proposition S1
.

Fix
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
and suppose that
(X,Y,O)\sim Q
. Let
\mathcal{O}_{Q}:=\{o\in\{0,1\}^{d}:\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o)>0\}
. If, for all
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, we have that
Y
and
O
are conditionally independent given
X^{\omega}
, then
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\mathcal{O}_{Q})
.

Fix
o\in\mathcal{O}_{Q}
and
\omega\preceq o
, then by Bayes’ Theorem and the assumed conditional independence we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{Q}(Y=1|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega},O=o)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o|Y=1,X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})\mathbb{P}_{Q}(Y=1|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}{\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{Q}(Y=1|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})

whenever
\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})>0
. On the other hand, if
\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=0
, then the right hand side in (
3
)
is conditioning on a null set under the distribution
Q
. Thus we can define a version of regression function satisfying

\mathbb{P}_{Q}(Y=1|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega},O=o)=\mathbb{P}_{Q}(Y=1|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}),

for all
o\in\mathcal{O}_{Q}
and
\omega\preceq o
and
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, without changing the distribution
Q
. This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma S2
.

Fix
S_{1},S_{2}\subseteq[d]
with
S_{1}\cap S_{2}=\emptyset
and
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
. Suppose that
(X,Y,O)\sim Q
, and let
\mathcal{O}_{Q}:=\{o\in\{0,1\}^{d}:\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o)>0\}
. Suppose further that the regression function
\eta(x)
depends on
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
only via
x_{S_{1}}:=\{x_{j}:j\in S_{1}\}
, and that
O\mid\{X=x,Y=y\}
depends on
(x,y)\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}
only via
x_{S_{2}}:=\{x_{j}:j\in S_{2}\}
. If
X_{S_{1}}
and
X_{S_{2}}
are independent, then
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\mathcal{O}_{Q})
.

First write
\omega^{\prime}=(\omega^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,\omega^{\prime}_{d})
with
\omega^{\prime}_{j}=1
if
j\in S_{1}
and
\omega^{\prime}_{j}=0
, otherwise. Similarly write
\omega^{\prime\prime}=(\omega^{\prime\prime}_{1},\ldots,\omega^{\prime\prime}_{d})
with
\omega^{\prime\prime}_{j}=1
if
j\in S_{2}
and
\omega^{\prime\prime}_{j}=0
otherwise.

Fix
\omega=(\omega_{1},\ldots,\omega_{d})\in\{0,1\}^{d}
. We then have that for
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X=x)\,d\mu_{\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}})\,d\mu_{\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X_{S_{1}}=x_{S_{1}})\,d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x)

Similarly, for
o\in\mathcal{O}_{Q}
with
o\preceq\omega
and
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, we have that

\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X_{S_{2}}=x_{S_{2}})\,d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime}}(x).

Now, since
X_{S_{1}}
and
X_{S_{2}}
are independent, we have that
X^{\omega^{\prime}}
and
X^{\omega^{\prime\prime}}
are independent and hence
X^{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime}}
and
X^{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime}}
are independent. It follows that, for
o\in\mathcal{O}_{Q}
with
o\preceq\omega
and
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(Y=1,O=o\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1,O=o\mid X=x)\,d\mu_{\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X=x)\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid Y=1,X=x)\,d\mu_{\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X_{S_{1}}=x_{S_{1}})\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X_{S_{2}}=x_{S_{2}})\,d\mu_{\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X_{S_{1}}=x_{S_{1}})\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X_{S_{2}}=x_{S_{2}})\,d\mu_{\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X_{S_{1}}\!=\!x_{S_{1}})\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X_{S_{2}}\!=\!z_{S_{2}})\,d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\,d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime}}(z^{\omega^{\prime\prime}})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X_{S_{1}}\!=\!x_{S_{1}})\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X_{S_{2}}\!=\!z_{S_{2}})\,d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x)\,d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime}}(z)

\displaystyle=\Bigl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(Y\!=\!1\mid X_{S_{1}}\!=\!x_{S_{1}})d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x)\Bigr\}\Bigl\{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{P}(O\!=\!o\mid X_{S_{2}}\!=\!z_{S_{2}})d\mu_{(\mathbf{1}_{d}-\omega)\wedge\omega^{\prime\prime}}(z)\Bigr\}.

It follows that

\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid O=o,X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=\frac{\mathbb{P}(Y=1,O=o\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}{\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}=\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})

as required.
∎

We now prove the claims made in the examples in Section
2

, as well as provide additional detail on the particular classes the corresponding distributions belong to.

We have
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=o)>0
for any
o\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, and thus
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\{0,1\}^{d})
by Proposition
S1
, since
(X,Y)
and
O
are independent. In the special case with
u=(a,0)^{T}
for some
a>0
, and
\Sigma=I
(in Setting 1 in Section
4

we have
a=2^{1/2})
), we will in fact show that

Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}^{+}\Bigl(\bigl\{(1,0)^{T}\bigr\},0.074,(\gamma_{1},1)^{T},C_{\mathrm{L}},\mathbf{1}_{2},1\vee(a/2),1,4/a,\{0,1\}^{2}\Bigr),

for any
\gamma_{1}\in[0,1)
and sufficiently large
C_{\mathrm{L}}
.

Indeed, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

\eta(x)=\frac{e^{-ax_{1}}}{e^{-ax_{1}}+e^{ax_{1}}}=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tanh(-ax_{1}).

Therefore, in the decomposition in (
6
)
the only nonzero term is
f_{(1,0)^{T}}(x)=\tanh(-ax_{1})/2
, and all other
f_{\omega}
s are zero. Further,

\displaystyle\sigma_{(1,0)^{T}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Q}\{\tanh^{2}(aX_{1})/4\}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x_{1}^{2}/2-a^{2}/2}\cosh(ax_{1})\tanh^{2}(ax_{1})\,dx_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4\sqrt{2\pi}}e^{-x_{1}^{2}/2-a^{2}/2-a^{2}x_{1}^{2}/2}\sinh^{2}(ax_{1})\,dx_{1}=\frac{e^{-a^{2}/2}\{e^{2a^{2}/(1+a^{2})}-1\}}{8(1+a^{2})^{1/2}},

where we used the inequality
\cosh(ax)\leq e^{a^{2}x^{2}/2}
. Thus, if for example
a=2^{1/2}
, then
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{(1,0)^{T}\},0.074,\{0,1\}^{2})
.

Turning now to the parameters in Definition
3

, first consider
\omega=(0,1)^{T}
, in this case the marginal distribution of
X_{2}
is a standard Gaussian random variable. Hence, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in\{0\}\times[2,\infty)
, we have by convexity

\displaystyle\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(x)

\displaystyle=\inf_{r\in(0,1)}\frac{\Phi(x_{2}+r)-\Phi(x_{2}-r)}{r}=2\phi(x_{2})

where
\Phi
and
\phi
denote the cumulative distribution function and density, respectively, of a standard Gaussian random variable. It follows that, for
\xi\leq\xi_{0}:=2\phi(2)
, we have

\mu_{\omega}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)\leq\mu_{\omega}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:2\phi(x_{2})<\xi\}\bigr)=2\Phi(-b)\leq e^{-b^{2}/2}=\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\xi}{\sqrt{2}},

where
b:=\Bigl(2\log(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi}\xi})\Bigr)^{1/2}
. On the other hand, for
\xi>\xi_{0}
, we have
\mu_{\omega}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)\leq 1\leq(\xi/\xi_{0})
. We conclude that we may take any
\gamma_{2}\in[0,1]
. Now, for
\omega=(1,0)^{T}
, the marginal distribution of
X_{1}
is a mixture of two Gaussian random variables with means
-a
and
a
, respectively. In this case, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}\times\{0\}
with
|x_{1}|>a+2
we have

\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(x)=\phi(x_{1}-a)+\phi(x_{1}+a)\geq 2\phi(|x_{1}|+a).

Then similarly to above, we have for
\xi\leq\xi_{1}:=2\phi(2a+2)
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\mu_{\omega}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:2\phi(|x_{1}|+a)<\xi\}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\Phi(-(c+a))+\Phi(-(c-a))\leq 2\Phi(-(c-a))

\displaystyle\leq e^{-(c-a)^{2}/2}=e^{-(c+a)^{2}/2+2ac}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\xi}{\sqrt{2}}\exp\Bigl(2a\sqrt{2\log(\sqrt{2}/(\sqrt{\pi}\xi))}\Bigr),

where
c:=\Bigl(2\log(\frac{\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\pi}\xi})\Bigr)^{1/2}-a
. On the other hand, for
\xi>\xi_{1}
, we have
\mu_{\omega}\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)\leq 1\leq(\xi/\xi_{1})^{\gamma_{1}}
, for any
\gamma_{1}<1
. Finally, by
Reeve et al., 2021
, we also deduce that

\mu\bigl(\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\rho_{\mu,2}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\xi^{\gamma_{1}}.

for any
\gamma_{1}<1
and some appropriate choice of
C_{\mathrm{L}}
. We therefore deduce that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}(\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\{0,1\}^{d})
, for any
\bm{\gamma}\in[0,1)\times[0,1]
and the corresponding choice of
C_{\mathrm{L}}
.

Regarding smoothness, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T},x^{\prime}=(x^{\prime}_{1},x^{\prime}_{2})^{T}\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, we have

|f_{(1,0)^{T}}(x)-f_{(1,0)^{T}}(x^{\prime})|=\frac{1}{2}|\tanh(-ax_{1})-\tanh(-ax^{\prime}_{1})|\leq\frac{a}{2}|x_{1}-x^{\prime}_{1}|,

and thus
P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{1}_{2},1\vee(a/2))
since all other
f_{\omega}
are
0
.

Finally, for
t>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P}(|\eta(X)-1/2|<t)

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{P}_{P}\Bigl\{0\leq\tanh(aX_{1})<2t\Bigr\}=2\mathbb{P}_{P}\Bigl\{0\leq X_{1}<\frac{\tanh^{-1}(2t)}{a}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\Phi\Bigl(2^{1/2}+\frac{\tanh^{-1}(2t)}{a}\Bigr)-\Phi\Bigl(a-\frac{\tanh^{-1}(2t)}{a}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\tanh^{-1}(2t)}{a}\leq\frac{4}{a}\cdot t.

It follows that
P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{M}}(1,1\vee 4/a)
.

∎

First, for
o\in\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\}
, we have that
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=o)>0
and no other observation patterns can be observed in the training data. Since
Y
and
O
are conditionally independent given
X^{\omega}
for all
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{4}
, we have
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\})
by Proposition
S1
.

In the special case used in Setting 2 in Section
4

, where for
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},x_{4})^{T}\in[0,1]^{4}
we have

\eta(x):=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{(x_{2}-1/2)\cdot x_{3}^{2}}{2}+\frac{x_{4}-1/2}{2},

the nonzero
f_{\omega}
functions are

f_{e_{2}}(x)=\frac{(x_{2}-1/2)}{6};\quad f_{e_{2}+e_{3}}(x)=\frac{(x_{2}-1/2)(x_{3}^{2}-1/3)}{2};\quad f_{e_{4}}(x)=\frac{(x_{4}-1/2)}{2}.

To see that
Y
and
O
are conditionally independent given
X^{\omega}
in this case, observe that for any
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
and
o=(1,1,1,0)^{T}
and writing
X=(X_{1},X_{2},X_{3},X_{4})^{T}
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\bigl(Y=1,O=o\bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl\{\mathbb{P}(Y=1,O=o\mid X)\Bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigl\{\mathbb{P}(Y=1\mid X)\cdot\mathbb{P}(O=o\mid X)\Bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\eta(X)\cdot X_{1}\bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigl\{\eta(X)\bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\bigr\}\cdot\mathbb{E}\bigl(X_{1}\bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\bigr),

since
\eta(x)
does not depend on
x_{1}
. If
o=(1,0,0,1)^{T}
, then similarly for any
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
we have that
\mathbb{P}(Y=1,O=o\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=\mathbb{E}\{\eta(X)\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\}\cdot\mathbb{E}(1-X_{1}\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})
.

We now show that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}
for appropriate choices of the parameters. For
\omega=(\omega_{1},\omega_{2},\omega_{3},\omega_{4})^{T}\in\{0,1\}^{4}
, we have
\mu_{\omega}=\otimes_{j=1}^{4}\bigl\{U([0,1])\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{j}=1\}}+\delta_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{j}=0\}}\bigr\}
, where
\delta_{0}
denotes a Dirac mass at
0
. Further, for
\omega\preceq(1,1,1,0)^{T}
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega|(1,1,1,0)^{T}}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{\mathrm{Beta}(2,1)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{1}=1\}}+\delta_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{1}=0\}}\bigr\}\otimes\bigl\{U([0,1])\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{2}=1\}}+\delta_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{2}=0\}}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\otimes\bigl\{U([0,1])\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{3}=1\}}+\delta_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{3}=0\}}\bigr\}\otimes\delta_{0}

and for
\omega\preceq(1,0,0,1)^{T}
, we have

\mu_{\omega|(1,0,0,1)^{T}}=\bigl\{\mathrm{Beta}(1,2)\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{1}=1\}}+\delta_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{1}=0\}}\bigr\}\otimes\delta_{0}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bigl\{U([0,1])\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{4}=1\}}+\delta_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega_{4}=0\}}\bigr\}.

To see this, note that for a measurable set
A\subseteq[0,1]^{3}
and
\omega=o=(1,1,1,0)^{T}
, we let
\tilde{A}=A\times\{0\}
and have

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega|o}(\tilde{A})

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(X^{\omega}\in\tilde{A}\mid O=o)=2\cdot\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=o\mid X^{\omega}\in\tilde{A})\cdot\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(X^{\omega}\in\tilde{A})

\displaystyle=2\cdot\int_{A}x_{1}dx_{1}\,dx_{2}\,dx_{3},

which proves the claim for
\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}}
, the other cases follows by similar calculations.

Now focusing on the nonzero
\sigma_{\omega}^{2}
for
\omega\in\Omega_{\star}
, we have

\sigma_{(0,1,1,0)^{T}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Bigl\{\frac{(X_{2}-1/2)^{2}(X_{3}^{2}-1/3)^{2}}{4}\Bigm|O=(1,1,1,0)^{T}\Bigr\}=\frac{1}{540}\approx 0.002,

\sigma_{(0,1,0,0)^{T}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Bigl\{\frac{(X_{2}-1/2)^{2}}{36}\Bigm|O=(1,1,1,0)^{T}\Bigr\}=\frac{1}{432}\approx 0.002

and

\sigma_{(0,0,0,1)^{T}}^{2}=\mathbb{E}\Bigl\{\frac{(X_{4}-1/2)^{2}}{4}\Bigm|O=(1,0,0,1)^{T}\Bigr\}=\frac{1}{48}\approx 0.02

therefore we have
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{(0,1,1,0)^{T},(0,0,0,1)^{T}\},1/540,\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\})
.

Turning to Definition
3

, consider first the lower density of
\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}}
. For
x_{1}\in[0,1]
and
r\in(0,x_{1}\wedge(1-x_{1}))
, we have

2\int_{x_{1}-r}^{x_{1}+r}x\,dx=\bigl\{(x_{1}+r)^{2}-(x_{1}-r)^{2}\bigr\}=4x_{1}r,

and for
0\leq x_{1}<r
we have

2\int_{0}^{(x_{1}+r)\wedge 1}x\,dx=(x_{1}+r)^{2}\wedge 1.

For
0<(1-x_{1})<r
we have that

2\int_{(x_{1}-r)\vee 0}^{1}x\,dx=1-\{(x_{1}-r)\vee 0\}^{2}.

It follows that, for
x_{1}\in[0,1]
, we have

\rho_{\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}},1}\bigl((x_{1},0,0,0)^{T}\bigr)=\inf_{r\in(0,1)}\frac{2\int_{(x_{1}-r)\vee 0}^{(x_{1}+r)\wedge 1}x\,dx}{r}=4x_{1}\wedge 1

A similar calculation shows that

\rho_{\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}|(1,0,0,1)^{T}},1}\bigl((x_{1},0,0,0)^{T}\bigr)=\inf_{r\in(0,1)}\frac{2\int_{(x_{1}-r)\vee 0}^{(x_{1}+r)\wedge 1}x\,dx}{r}=\{4(1-x_{1})\}\wedge 1,

for
x_{1}\in[0,1]
. Therefore, for
\xi<1
, we have

\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in[0,1]^{4}:\min_{o\in\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\}}\rho_{\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}|o},1}(x)<\xi\Bigr\}\Bigr)=\xi/2\leq\xi,

whereas, for
\xi\geq 1
, we have

\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in[0,1]^{4}:\min_{o\in\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\}}\rho_{\mu_{(1,0,0,0)^{T}|o},1}(x)<\xi\Bigr\}\Bigr)=1\leq\xi.

For
\omega\in\{e_{2},e_{3},e_{4}\}
, and
o\in\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\}
with
\omega\preceq o
, we have
\mu_{\omega}=\mu_{\omega|o}
and thus
\rho_{\mu_{\omega},1}(\cdot)=1
on the support of
X^{\omega}
, and

\mu_{\omega}\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in[0,1]^{4}:\min_{o\in\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\}}\rho_{\mu_{\omega|o},1}(x)<\xi\Bigr\}\Bigr)=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\xi>1\}}\leq\xi^{\gamma}

for any choice of
\gamma\geq 0
. For
\omega=o=(1,1,1,0)^{T}
, we have that
\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}}=\mathrm{Beta}(2,1)\otimes U([0,1])^{\otimes 2}\otimes\delta_{0}
. Thus, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3},0)^{T}\in[0,1]^{3}\times\{0\}
, since
\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:\|z-(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{T}\|_{\infty}<r/\sqrt{3}\}\subseteq\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\|z-(x_{1},x_{2},x_{3})^{T}\|<r\}
, we have

\rho_{\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}},3}(x)\geq\inf_{r\in(0,1)}\frac{\nu((x_{1}-r/\sqrt{3},x_{1}+r/\sqrt{3}))\cdot r^{2}/3}{r^{3}}\geq\frac{(4x_{1}\wedge 1)}{3\sqrt{3}},

where
\nu
denotes the measure of a
\mathrm{Beta}(2,1)
distribution. Thus, for
\xi<1/(3\sqrt{3})
, we have

\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in[0,1]^{4}:\rho_{\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}},3}(x)<\xi\Bigr\}\Bigr)\leq\frac{2\xi}{3^{3/2}}.

For
\xi\geq 1/(3\sqrt{3})
we have

\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in[0,1]^{4}:\rho_{\mu_{(1,1,1,0)^{T}|(1,1,1,0)^{T}},2}(x)<\xi\Bigr\}\Bigr)\leq 1\leq 3^{3/2}\xi.

For
\omega\prec o=(1,1,1,0)^{T}
, and
\omega\preceq o=(1,0,0,1)^{T}
similar calculations show that (
9
)
holds when
\bm{\gamma}=(1,\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},\gamma_{4})\in\{1\}\times[0,\infty)^{3}
. Therefore

Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}\bigl((1,\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},\gamma_{4})^{T},3^{3/2},\{(1,1,1,0)^{T},(1,0,0,1)^{T}\}\bigr)

for any
\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3},\gamma_{4}\in[0,\infty)
. Further, similar (and slightly simpler) calculations confirm that in fact
Q
belongs to the
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}^{+}
class with the same parameters.

In terms of smoothness and the margin parameter, we have
P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{1}_{4},1)
and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{P}

\displaystyle\Bigl(|\eta(X)-1/2|<t\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\mathbb{P}_{P}\Bigl(|\eta(X)-1/2|<t\Bigm|X_{1}=x_{1},X_{2}=x_{2},X_{3}=x_{3}\Bigr)\,dx_{1}\,dx_{2}\,dx_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{-2t-1/2-(x_{2}-1/2)x_{3}^{2}}^{2t-1/2-(x_{2}-1/2)x_{3}^{2}}\,dx_{4}\,dx_{1}\,dx_{2}\,dx_{3}=4t,

thus
P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{M}}(1,4)
.
∎

We have
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=o)>0
for any
o\in\{0,1\}^{2}
, and further
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=o|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})>0
for any
o,\omega\in\{0,1\}^{2}
and
x\in[0,1]^{2}
.

First, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in[0,1]^{2}
with
x_{2}\leq 1/2
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, we have that
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega},Y=1)=1/2=\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega},Y=0)
, and thus
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega},Y=1)=1/2
. It follows that, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in[0,1]^{2}
with
x_{2}\leq 1/2
and
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega},O=\mathbf{1}_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\mid Y=1,X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})\cdot\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}{\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}

\displaystyle=\frac{1/2\cdot\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})}{1/2}=\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}).

Similarly, for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in[0,1]^{2}
with
x_{2}\leq 1/2
,
r\in\{0,1\}
, and
o,\omega\in\{0,1\}^{2}\setminus\{\mathbf{1}_{2}\}
with
\omega\preceq o
, we have that
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}_{Q}(O=o\mid Y=r,X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=1/6
. Therefore (
3
)
holds for
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in[0,1]^{2}
with
x_{2}\leq 1/2
. Similar calculations show that (
3
)
also holds
x=(x_{1},x_{2})^{T}\in[0,1]^{2}
with
x_{2}>1/2
, and we deduce that
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\{0,1\}^{2})
.

Regarding the class
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}
, we recall that
\eta(x)=1/4+x_{1}/2+1/4\cdot\cos(4\pi x_{2})
and thus the nonzero
f_{\omega}
functions are

f_{e_{1}}=\frac{x_{1}-1/2}{2};\qquad f_{e_{2}}=\frac{\cos(4\pi x_{2})}{4}.

We therefore have

\displaystyle\sigma_{e_{1}}^{2}=\min\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl\{f_{e_{1}}^{2}(X)\bigm|O=e_{1}\bigr\},\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl\{f_{e_{1}}^{2}(X)\bigm|O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\bigr\}\Bigr\}=1/48\approx 0.02

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{e_{2}}^{2}=\min\Bigl\{\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl\{f_{e_{2}}^{2}(X)\bigm|O=e_{2}\bigr\},\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl\{f_{e_{2}}^{2}(X)\bigm|O=\mathbf{1}_{2}\bigr\}\Bigr\}=1/32\approx 0.03,

and thus
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{e_{1},e_{2}\},1/48,\{0,1\}^{2})
.

Further, in this example, all relevant lower densities are bounded below by a universal constant, and therefore
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}^{+}(\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\{0,1\}^{2})
,
for any
\bm{\gamma}\in[0,\infty)^{2}
and sufficiently large
C_{\mathrm{L}}\geq 1
(depending on
\bm{\gamma}
and the lower bound on the lower densities). Finally, we have
P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{1}_{2},\pi)
and
P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{M}}(1,8)
, since

\mathbb{P}_{P}(|\eta(X)-1/2|\leq t)\leq\int_{0}^{1}\int_{1/2-4t-\cos(4\pi x_{2})}^{1/2+4t-\cos(4\pi x_{2})}\,dx_{1}\,dx_{2}\leq 8t.

∎

S1.2
Properties of the regression function and its anova decomposition

Proposition S3
.

Fix
Q\in\mathcal{Q}
. For any
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, we have for the conditional regression functions
\eta_{\omega}(x):=\mathbb{P}_{P}(Y=1\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})
that

\displaystyle\eta_{\omega}(\cdot)=\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\preceq\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(\cdot).

In particular,
\eta(\cdot)=\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}}f_{\omega}(\cdot)
.

Fix an observation pattern
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
and
x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}
, and recall that

f_{\omega}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{\eta(X)-\frac{1}{2}-\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\prec\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(X)\Bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\eta_{\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{P}(Y\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{Q}\bigl\{\eta(X)\mid X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{P}\Bigl\{\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\prec\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(X)\Bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}+f_{\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\preceq\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega})=\frac{1}{2}+\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\preceq\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(x).

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition S4
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
. There exists a constant
C_{\mathrm{B}}\geq 1
only depending on
d
such that for any
x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}
, any
P\in\mathcal{P}
, and the estimators
\hat{f}_{\omega}
for
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
defined in Section
3

we have
|f_{\omega}(x)|\leq C_{\mathrm{B}}
,
|\hat{f}_{\omega}(x)|\leq C_{\mathrm{B}}
, and
|\hat{f}_{\omega}(x)-f_{\omega}(x)|\leq 2C_{\mathrm{B}}
, for all
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}
. In particular, we may take
C_{\mathrm{B}}
to be the
d
th ordered Bell number.

We will in fact show that
\|f_{\omega}\|_{\infty}
and
\|\hat{f}_{\omega}\|_{\infty}
are bounded by the
d_{\omega}
th ordered Bell number
B_{d_{\omega}}:=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{j^{d_{\omega}}}{2^{j}}
, which are increasing and satisfy the recurrence relation that
B_{0}=B_{1}=1
and
B_{k}=1+\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\binom{k}{j}B_{j}
, for
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Now, since
\eta(x)\in[0,1]
for all
x
, we have
f_{\mathbf{0}_{d}}=\mathbb{E}_{P}\{\eta(X)\}-1/2\in[-1/2,1/2]
and
|f_{\mathbf{0}_{d}}|\leq 1/2<B_{0}
. By induction over
d_{\omega}
, suppose that
\|f_{\omega^{\prime}}\|_{\infty}\leq B_{d_{\omega^{\prime}}}
for
\omega^{\prime}\preceq\omega
, then we have that

\displaystyle|f_{\omega}(x)|

\displaystyle=\Bigl|\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{\eta(X)-\frac{1}{2}-\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\prec\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(X)\Bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}\Bigr|

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{|\eta(X)-1/2-f_{\mathbf{0}_{d}}|+\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\omega}-1}\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\prec\omega:d_{\omega^{\prime}}=j}|f_{\omega^{\prime}}(X)|\Bigm|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq 1+\sum_{j=1}^{d_{\omega}-1}\binom{d_{\omega}}{j}B_{j}=:B_{d_{\omega}}.

This proves the result for
f_{\omega}(x)
. Similar steps using (
S16
) give a bound for the estimator
\hat{f}_{\omega}
. Finally,
|\hat{f}_{\omega}(x)-f_{\omega}(x)|\leq|\hat{f}_{\omega}(x)|+|f_{\omega}(x)|\leq 2C_{\mathrm{B}}
.
∎

S2
Technical results and additional lemmas for the proof of the lower bound in Theorem
1

The proof of the lower bound in Theorem
1

makes use of several intermediate results. The first in Section
S2.1
is a version of Assouad’s Lemma (Lemma
S5
), modified to our missing data setting. The remainder of the proof involves constructing a set of distributions belonging to
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}^{\prime}
for which we can apply Assouad’s Lemma and obtain the desired lower bound. The proof is separated into the light and heavy tailed settings, and begins with the case that
\Omega_{\star}=\{\omega\}
. For the light tailed case, the marginal constructions are given in Section
5.1

in the main text. Section
S2.2
establishes that the corresponding distributions belong to the class
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}(\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\mathcal{O})
, Section
S2.3
defines the associated regression functions, and establishes that the corresponding distributions belong to the classes
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{\omega\},c_{\mathrm{E}},\mathcal{O})
,
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\bm{\beta},C_{\mathrm{S}})
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{M}}(\alpha,C_{\mathrm{M}})
. The proofs for the special case when
\Omega_{\star}
is a singleton in the light tailed case is completed in Section
S2.4
. The corresponding arguments for the heavy tailed case are presented in Sections 5.1.2,
S2.5
,
S2.6
and
S2.7
. Finally, the proof for general
\Omega_{\star}
, which builds on the earlier arguments, is presented in Section
S2.8
.

S2.1
Assouad’s Lemma for classification with missing data

Lemma S5
(Assouad).

Fix
d,n,m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\omega,o_{1},\ldots,o_{n}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
and
r>0
. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a set of distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}
, let
\Sigma=\{-1,1\}^{m}
,
u\in[0,1/(m\cdot 2^{d_{\omega}})]
, and
\epsilon\in[0,1/4]
. Fix
z_{1},\ldots,z_{m}\in(0,r)^{d}
satisfying
z_{i}^{\omega}\neq z_{j}^{\omega}
for
i\neq j
. Assume that the class of distributions
\{Q^{\sigma}:\sigma\in\Sigma\}\subseteq\mathcal{Q}
, which have associated marginal distributions
P^{\sigma}
of
(X,Y)
, regression functions
\eta^{\sigma}:\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}\rightarrow[0,1]
and each with
X
-marginal
\mu
, satisfy

(i)

2^{d_{\omega}+5}n_{\omega}\epsilon^{2}u\leq 1
, where
n_{\omega}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega\preceq o_{i}\}}
;

(ii)

we have
\mu(\{s^{\omega}\odot z_{t}^{\omega}\})=u
for all
t\in[m]
and
s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
;

(iii)

for
\sigma=(\sigma_{t})_{t\in[m]}\in\Sigma
, we have
\eta^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t}^{\omega})=1/2+\bigl(\prod_{j\in[d]:(s^{\omega})_{j}=-1}(s^{\omega})_{j}\bigr)\cdot\sigma_{t}\epsilon
, for all
t\in[m]
and
s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
, in particular we have
\eta^{\sigma}(z^{\omega}_{t})=1/2+\sigma_{t}\epsilon
, for
t\in[m]
;

(iv)

for
\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\Sigma
, we have
\eta^{\sigma}(x)=\eta^{\sigma^{\prime}}(x)
for all
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\setminus\bigcup_{t\in[m],s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\{s^{\omega}\odot z_{t}^{\omega}\}
;

(v)

we have
\Bigl(\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\setminus\bigcup_{t\in[m],s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\{s^{\omega}\odot z_{t}^{\omega}\}\Bigr)\bigcap(-r-1,r+1)^{d}=\emptyset
;

(vi)

for
\sigma\in\Sigma
, if
(X,Y,O)\sim Q^{\sigma}
, then
O\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}(X,Y)
.

Then

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{C}\in\mathcal{C}}\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P_{Q}}(\hat{C})\bigm|O_{1}=o_{1},\ldots,O_{n}=o_{n}\bigr\}\geq 2^{d_{\omega}-1}m\epsilon u.

(S1)

For
\sigma=(\sigma_{t})_{t\in[m]}\in\Sigma
and
t^{\prime}\in[m]
, define
\sigma^{\prime}\equiv\sigma(t^{\prime}):=(\sigma_{t}(t^{\prime}))_{t\in[m]}\in\Sigma
, with
\sigma_{t}(t^{\prime}):=-\sigma_{t}
if
t^{\prime}=t
, and
\sigma_{t}(t^{\prime}):=\sigma_{t}
otherwise. Then, for
s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
, we have
\eta^{\sigma^{\prime}}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})=1-\eta^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})
and
\eta^{\sigma^{\prime}}(x)=\eta^{\sigma}(x)
for
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\setminus\bigcup_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\{s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega}\}
. Now, for
\omega_{0}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, let
P^{\sigma}_{\omega_{0}}
denote the marginal distribution of
(X^{\omega_{0}},Y)
when
(X,Y)\sim P^{\sigma}
. By
(vi)
, we have that
P^{\sigma}_{\omega_{0}}=P^{\sigma}_{\omega_{0}|o}
, for
o\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, where
P^{\sigma}_{\omega_{0}|o}
denotes the conditional distribution of
(X^{\omega_{0}},Y)|\{O=o\}
when
(X,Y,O)\sim Q^{\sigma}
. For
\omega\preceq\omega_{0}
and
x\in(-r-1,r+1)^{d}\cap\mathrm{supp}(\mu)
, we have that

\eta^{\sigma}_{\omega_{0}}(x):=\mathbb{E}_{P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}}(Y|X^{\omega_{0}}=x^{\omega_{0}})=\mathbb{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(Y|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega})=\eta^{\sigma}(x).

Thus, for
\omega\preceq\omega_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{KL}(

\displaystyle P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma},P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle:=\!\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\omega_{0}}}}\Bigl[\eta_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}(x^{\omega_{0}})\log\Bigl(\frac{\eta_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}(x)}{\eta_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}(x)}\Bigr)+\{1-\eta_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}(x^{\omega_{0}})\}\log\Bigl(\frac{1-\eta_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}(x^{\omega_{0}})}{1-\eta_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}(x^{\omega_{0}})}\Bigr)\Bigr]\,d\mu_{\omega_{0}}(x^{\omega_{0}})

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=\frac{1}{2^{d-d_{\omega}}}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\bigl\{2\eta^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})-1\bigr\}\log\Bigl(\frac{\eta^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})}{1-\eta^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})}\Big)\cdot\mu(\{s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega}\})

\displaystyle\phantom{:}=2^{d_{\omega}+1}\epsilon\sigma_{t^{\prime}}\cdot\log\Bigl(\frac{1+2\epsilon\sigma_{t^{\prime}}}{1-2\epsilon\sigma_{t^{\prime}}}\Bigr)\cdot u\leq\frac{2^{d_{\omega}+3}\epsilon^{2}u}{1-2\epsilon}\leq 2^{d_{\omega}+4}\epsilon^{2}u,

where the penultimate inequality uses the inequality
\log a\leq a-1
for
a\geq 1
. On the other hand, if
\omega\npreceq\omega_{0}
, then

\eta^{\sigma}_{\omega_{0}}(x)=\mathbb{E}_{P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}}(Y|X^{\omega\wedge\omega_{0}}=x^{\omega\wedge\omega_{0}})=1/2,

and therefore
P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma}=P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma^{\prime}}
, and in particular
\mathrm{KL}(P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma},P_{\omega_{0}}^{\sigma^{\prime}})=0
. Then, writing
\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}:=\bigotimes_{i\in[n]}P^{\sigma}_{o_{i}}
, by Pinsker’s inequality and (i), we have that

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\Pi_{n}^{\sigma},\Pi_{n}^{\sigma^{\prime}}\bigr)\leq\sqrt{\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathrm{KL}(P_{o_{i}}^{\sigma},P_{o_{i}}^{\sigma^{\prime}})}{2}}\leq\sqrt{n_{\omega}2^{d_{\omega}+3}\epsilon^{2}u}\leq 1/2.

Now, for
\hat{C}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
and
\sigma\in\Sigma
, let
C^{\sigma}(x):=\mathbbm{1}_{\{\eta^{\sigma}(x)\geq 1/2\}}
be the Bayes classifier under
P^{\sigma}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\hat{C})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{C}(x)\neq C^{\sigma}(x)\}}|2\eta^{\sigma}(x)-1|\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2\epsilon u}{2^{d-d_{\omega}}}\sum_{t\in[m]}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\hat{C}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t})\neq C^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t})\}}.

Further, for
\hat{C}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
, we write
\tilde{C}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\})^{n}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
when
\hat{C}
is applied with the observation indicators being equal to
o_{1},\ldots,o_{n}
, i.e.

\tilde{C}(x,(x_{1}^{o_{1}},y_{1}),\ldots,(x_{n}^{o_{n}},y_{n}))=\hat{C}(x,(x_{1}^{o_{1}},y_{1},o_{1}),\ldots,(x_{n}^{o_{n}},y_{n},o_{n}))

Then, using
(vi)
,

\mathbb{E}_{Q^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\hat{C})\bigm|O_{1}=o_{1},\ldots,O_{n}=o_{n}\bigr\}=\mathbb{E}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}.

Thus

\mathbb{E}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}\geq\frac{2\epsilon u}{2^{d-d_{\omega}}}\sum_{t\in[m]}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\mathbb{P}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\Bigl\{\tilde{C}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t})\neq C^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t})\Bigr\}.

It follows that, for
t^{\prime}\in[m]
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma(t^{\prime})}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma(t^{\prime})}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2^{d_{\omega}+1}\epsilon u}{2^{d}}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}\Bigl[\mathbb{P}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\Bigl\{\tilde{C}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t^{\prime}})\neq C^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t^{\prime}})\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\hskip 40.0pt+\mathbb{P}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma(t^{\prime})}}\Bigl\{\tilde{C}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t^{\prime}})\neq C^{\sigma(t^{\prime})}(s^{\omega}\odot z^{\omega}_{t^{\prime}})\Bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\geq 2^{d_{\omega}+1}\epsilon u\Bigl\{1-\mathrm{TV}\bigl(\Pi^{\sigma}_{n},\Pi^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{n}\bigr)\Bigr\}\geq 2^{d_{\omega}}\epsilon u,

since
C^{\sigma}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})=1-C^{\sigma(t^{\prime})}(s^{\omega}\odot z_{t^{\prime}}^{\omega})
. We conclude that, for
\hat{C}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathbb{E}_{Q}\bigl\{

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{P_{Q}}(\hat{C})\bigm|O_{1}=o_{1},\ldots,O_{n}=o_{n}\bigr\}

\displaystyle\geq\max_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}\geq\frac{1}{2^{m}}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma}\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{m}}\sum_{t\in[m]}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma:\sigma_{t}=1}\Bigl[\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}+\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{\Pi_{n}^{\sigma(t)}}\bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P^{\sigma(t)}}(\tilde{C})\bigr\}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2^{d_{\omega}}}{2^{m}}\sum_{t\in[m]}\sum_{\sigma\in\Sigma:\sigma_{t}=1}\epsilon u=2^{d_{\omega}-1}m\epsilon u,

as required.
∎

S2.2
Properties of the marginal construction in the light tailed case

Recall the construction of
\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
given in Section
5.1.1

. We show in this section that this marginal feature distribution indeed falls into our proposed class of distributions
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}(\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\mathcal{O})
. We start with three technical lemmas.

Lemma S6
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
r>0
, and
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
. For any
x\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
we have that

\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)\geq\kappa^{d_{\omega}}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}

for all
\zeta\in[0,1]
. For any
x\in\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
we have that

\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)\geq r^{d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}

for all
\zeta\in[0,1]
.

First, fix
x\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
. For
\zeta\in[0,1]
, consider the map on
\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}
given by

\psi_{x,\zeta}:\tilde{x}\mapsto\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x)+\zeta\cdot\Bigl(\tilde{x}-\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x)\Bigr).

Observe that
\psi_{x,\zeta}(\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{R}^{\omega}))\subset\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{R}^{\omega})
. Furthermore, since

\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})=\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}})\in\psi_{x,\zeta}(\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{R}^{\omega}))

and
\mathrm{diam}(\psi_{x,\zeta}(\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{R}^{\omega}))\leq\zeta\kappa d^{1/2}_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}<\zeta
,
we also have
\psi_{x,\zeta}(\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{R}^{\omega}))\subseteq\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\})
. We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(

\displaystyle\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\kappa^{d_{\omega}-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}\Bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\Bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\kappa^{d_{\omega}-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\cdot\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\bigl(\psi_{x,\zeta}(\Pi_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}(\mathcal{R}^{\omega}))\bigr)\geq\kappa^{d_{\omega}}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}.

This concludes the proof of the first part of the lemma.

For the second part, fix
x\in\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
. If
\zeta\in[0,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})\wedge 1]
, then there exists a
d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}
-dimensional hypercube
A
with side length
\zeta/\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}
such that
A\subseteq\Pi_{\omega^{\prime}\wedge\omega^{\prime}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}_{j}\bigr)\Bigr)
, thus for
\zeta\in[0,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})\wedge 1]
,

\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)\geq d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}/2}\cdot r^{d_{\omega}-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}

If
\zeta\in[(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})\wedge 1,1]
, then
\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\subseteq\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\bigr)
, and

\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)=r^{d_{\omega}}\geq r^{d_{\omega}}\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}.

The result follows.
∎

Lemma S7
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
r>0
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
x\in\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega}
, and
\zeta>0
.
If
\zeta\leq 4r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}
, then we have that

|\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}\cap B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})|\geq\Bigl(\frac{q\zeta}{8r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\Bigr)^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}.

First observe that since
x\in\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega}
, we have
x^{\omega^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}
. If
q=1
, then
|\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}\cap B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})|=1\geq(\zeta/(8r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}))^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}
. Now if
q\geq 2
, since
\frac{\zeta}{8\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\leq\frac{r}{2}
we can find a
d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}
-dimensional, axis-aligned hypercube
A
with vertex
x^{\omega^{\prime}}
and side length
\zeta/(8\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})
containing at least
\lceil q\zeta/(8r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})\rceil^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}
elements of
\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}
. We conclude that
|\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}\cap B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})|\geq|\mathcal{T}_{q,r,\tilde{r}}^{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}\cap A|\geq\bigl(\frac{q\zeta}{8r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\bigr)^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}
.
∎

Now recall the definition of the lower density
\rho_{\nu,s}
from (
8
)
. For
\mu\equiv\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, we’ll write
\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}
in place of
\rho_{(\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b})_{\omega^{\prime}},d_{\omega^{\prime}}}
. Our next result derives lower bounds on
\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}
in different regions of the feature space.

Lemma S8
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
r>0
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
, and
a,b\in[0,1/4]
. Then, for
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
, such that
\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{0}_{d}
, we have

(i)

\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\geq\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}
for all
x\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
,

(ii)

\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\geq\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigr\}
for all
x\in\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
and
j\in[d]
with
\omega_{j}=1
,

(iii)

if
\omega\npreceq\omega^{\prime}
, then
\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\geq\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigr\}
for all
x\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}
,

(iv)

if
\omega\preceq\omega^{\prime}
, then
\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\geq 2^{-3d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,a(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}
for all
x\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}
.

To prove (i), fix
x\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
,
\zeta\in(0,1)
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
. Then, by Lemma
S6
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mu\Bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(1-a-b)2^{d-d_{\omega}}}{2^{d}\kappa^{d_{\omega}}}\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}.

(S2)

Hence

\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})=\inf_{\zeta\in(0,1)}\frac{\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)}{\zeta^{d_{\omega^{\prime}}}}\geq\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\cdot\inf_{\zeta\in(0,1)}\frac{1}{\zeta^{d_{\omega^{\prime}}-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}=\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}.

To prove (ii), fix
x\in\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
,
\zeta\in(0,1)
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
. Then, by Lemma
S6
, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mu\Bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b2^{d-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}{2^{d}d_{\omega}r^{d_{\omega}}}\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}d_{\omega}r^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot r^{d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

The result of part (ii) follows via the same argument used in part (i).

To proof (iii), fix
x\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
, with
\omega\npreceq\omega^{\prime}
. Note that for any
x\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}
there exists a
j\in[d]
such that
\omega_{j}=1
and
\tilde{x}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}_{j}
such that
\tilde{x}^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}}
, and we have by Lemma
S6

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mu\Bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\Bigr)=\mu\Bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(\tilde{x}^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b2^{d-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}{2^{d}d_{\omega}r^{d_{\omega}}}\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(\tilde{x}^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\cap\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}d_{\omega}r^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot r^{d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

To prove (iv), fix
x\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}
and
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
, with
\omega\preceq\omega^{\prime}
. If
\zeta\in(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}},1]
, then letting
z:=\bigl(1+2r,\ldots,1+2r)
, we have
z^{\omega}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
and
\|x^{\omega}-z^{\omega}\|\leq 2r\sqrt{d_{\omega}}<\zeta/2
. Hence, by (
S2
), we have

\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)\geq\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta/2}(z^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)\geq\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}(\zeta/2)^{d_{\omega}}\geq 2^{-3d_{\omega}}\cdot\zeta^{d_{\omega}}.

On the other hand, if
\zeta\in(0,(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})\wedge 1]

we have by Lemma
S7
,

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mu\Bigl(\bigl\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:z^{\omega^{\prime}}\in B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr\}\Bigr)\geq\frac{a}{(2q)^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot\bigl|\mathcal{T}_{q,r}^{\omega}\cap B_{\zeta}(x^{\omega^{\prime}})\bigr|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{a}{(2q)^{d_{\omega}}}\Bigl(\frac{q\zeta}{8r\sqrt{d_{\omega}}}\Bigr)^{d_{\omega}}\geq\frac{a}{2^{3d_{\omega}}}\Bigl(\frac{1}{4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}}}\Bigr)^{d_{\omega}}\zeta^{d_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

∎

The next corollary exploits the previous lemmas and gives conditions under which a marginal distribution of the type (
15
)
falls into the class
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}(\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\mathcal{O})
.

Corollary S9
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\{0,1\}^{d}
,
\bm{\gamma}=(\gamma_{1},\dots,\gamma_{d})^{T}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
,
r>0
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
and
a,b\in[0,1/4]
. Let

\gamma_{\omega}:=\min\{\gamma_{j}:\omega_{j}=1\}
and let
\gamma_{\max}:=\max\{\gamma_{j}:j\in[d]\}
. Let
a_{0}:=2^{-3d_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}}
and
b_{0}=2^{-1}(d_{\omega}2^{d_{\omega}})^{-\gamma_{\max}}
. Fix
C_{\mathrm{L}}>4^{\gamma_{\max}(d_{\omega}+1)}
. Let
Q\equiv Q_{\omega,\kappa,r,q,a,b}
denote a distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}^{d}
with corresponding
X
-marginal distribution
\mu=\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
, that also satisfies
\min_{o\in\mathcal{O}}\mathbb{P}_{Q}(O=o)>0
and for which
O\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}(X,Y)
, when
(X,Y,O)\sim Q
. If
a\leq a_{0}\wedge b
and

\displaystyle a^{1-\gamma_{\omega}}(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{d_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}}\leq a_{0},

(S3)

\displaystyle b^{1-\gamma_{\omega}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot b_{0}\cdot\Bigl(\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\omega}},

(S4)

\displaystyle b^{1-\gamma_{\max}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot b_{0}\cdot\Bigl(\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\max}},

(S5)

for all
\omega^{\prime}
with
\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{0}_{d}
, then
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{L}}(\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\mathcal{O})
.

First, since
O
is independent of
(X,Y)
, we have
\mu_{\omega^{\prime}\mid o}=\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}
and thus, for any
\omega^{\prime},o\in\{0,1\}^{d}
and
x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}
,
\rho_{\mu_{\omega^{\prime}\mid o},d_{\omega^{\prime}}}(x)=\rho_{\mu_{\omega^{\prime}},d_{\omega^{\prime}}}(x)
. Therefore it suffices to show that (
9
)
holds with
\rho_{\mu_{\omega^{\prime}},d_{\omega^{\prime}}}(x)
instead of
\min_{o\in\mathcal{O}:\omega^{\prime}\preceq o}\rho_{\mu_{\omega^{\prime}\mid o},d_{\omega^{\prime}}}(x)
.

Now fix
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
. First, if
\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}
, then (
9
)
holds with
C_{L}=1
and any
\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}>0
, since
\mu_{\mathbf{0}}
is a point mass at
\mathbf{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. If
\omega\preceq\omega^{\prime}
and

\xi\in\Biggl(0,\Bigl(2^{-3d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,a(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)\wedge\Bigl(\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)\Biggr],

then by the symmetry of
\mu
and Lemma
S8
, for any
x\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\cup\bigcup_{j\in[d_{\omega}]}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
and
s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
, we have
\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(s\odot x^{\omega^{\prime}})>\xi
. It follows that

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)=0\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

Next, if
2^{-3d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,a(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}<\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}
and

\xi\in\Biggl(2^{-3d_{\omega}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,a(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\},\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}\Biggr],

then, by (
S3
), we have

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq a\leq 2^{-3d_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,a^{\gamma_{\omega}}(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

If
\xi\in\Biggl(\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\},\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega}}}\Biggr]
, then we have by (
S4
) and since
a\leq b
, that

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq a+b\leq 2b\leq 2C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot b_{0}\cdot\Bigl(b\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\Bigl(\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{-d_{\omega}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

If
\xi>\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega}}}
, then we have

\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)\leq 1=1^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}\leq 2^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}(d_{\omega}+1)}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}

since
1-a-b>1/2
.

Now consider
\omega\npreceq\omega^{\prime}
satisfying
\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{0}_{d}
. First for

\xi\in\Biggl(0,\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigr\}\Biggr]

we have, similarly to above, that

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)=0\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

If

\xi\in\Biggl(\frac{b}{d_{\omega}\cdot 2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigr\},\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\Biggr],

then, by (
S5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)

\displaystyle\leq a+b\leq 2b

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\Bigl(\frac{b}{d_{\omega}2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}\cdot\min\Bigl\{1,(r\sqrt{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}})^{-d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}\Bigr\}\Bigr)^{\gamma_{\max}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\max}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}.

Finally, if
\xi>\frac{1-a-b}{2^{d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}}}
, then we have

\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\bigl(\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:\rho_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)<\xi\}\bigr)\leq 1=1^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}\leq 2^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}(d_{\omega\wedge\omega^{\prime}}+1)}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{L}}\cdot\xi^{\gamma_{\omega^{\prime}}}

since
1-a-b>1/2
. This completes the proof.
∎

S2.3
Regression function construction in the light tailed case

We now construct different regression functions
\eta_{\omega}^{\sigma}
, for
\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{T}
, which will satisfy the assumptions of our version of Assouad’s Lemma. To this end, we will use the marginal distribution
\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
constructed in Section
5.1.1

, along with the additional quantities
\bm{\beta}=(\beta_{1},\ldots,\beta_{d})\in(0,1]^{d}
,
c_{\mathrm{E}}\in[0,1/4]
,
\epsilon\in(0,1/4)
and
\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{T}
. Let
\mathcal{S}^{\omega}=\{x^{\omega}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:(\Pi_{\omega}(x^{\omega}))_{j}=0
for some
j\in[d_{\omega}]\}
be the coordinate axes lying in the
d_{\omega}
-dimensional submanifold
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{\omega}=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:x_{j}=0
for all
j\in[d]
with
\omega_{j}=0\}
. Recalling also that
z:=(1+2r,\ldots,1+2r)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we define the function
f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}:\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\cup\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\omega}\cup\bigcup_{j\in[d]:\omega_{j}=1}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}\rightarrow\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}
by

\displaystyle f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x):=\begin{cases}\epsilon+\frac{1}{4}\lVert x^{\omega}-z^{\omega}\rVert^{\beta_{\omega}}&\mbox{if }x^{\omega}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\\
\sigma_{t}\cdot\epsilon&\mbox{if }x^{\omega}=x^{\omega}_{t}\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\\
0&\mbox{if }x^{\omega}\in\mathcal{S}^{\omega}\\
\frac{1}{2}&\mbox{if }x^{\omega}\in\bigcup_{j\in[d]:\omega_{j}=1}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}.\end{cases}

(S6)

Further, we claim there exists a
\beta_{\omega}
-Hölder continuous extension
f_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
of
f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
onto
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Let
Q
be the distribution of
(X,Y,O)
with
O\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}(X,Y)
,
X
-marginal
\mu=\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
and regression function
\eta^{\sigma}(x)=1/2+f_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)
, then
Q
belongs to
\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{\omega\},c_{\mathrm{E}},\mathcal{O})
, and
P\equiv P_{Q}
falls into the classes
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\bm{\beta},C_{\mathrm{S}})
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{M}}(\alpha,C_{\mathrm{M}})
for appropriate choices of
C_{\mathrm{S}}
,
\alpha
,
C_{\mathrm{M}}
and
\mathcal{O}
. This is made precise in the next three lemmas.

Lemma S10
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>0
,
\beta_{\omega}\in(0,1]
,
\epsilon\in(0,(1/4)\wedge(1/8\cdot(r/q)^{\beta_{\omega}})]
. Let further
\sigma=(\sigma_{t})_{t\in[T]}\in\{-1,1\}^{T}
. Then

\displaystyle|f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}},

for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\cup\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\omega}\cup\bigcup_{j\in[d]:\omega_{j}=1}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
.

If
x_{1}=x_{2}
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{S}^{\omega}
, or
x_{1},x_{2}\in\bigcup_{j\in[d]:\omega_{j}=1}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
, then
|f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|=0\leq 1/2\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}
. If
x_{1}\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\omega}
and
x_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}
, with
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
, then

|f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|\leq 2\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{4}(r/q)^{\beta_{\omega}}\leq\frac{1}{4}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}.

If
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
, then, by Minkowski’s inequality,

|f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|=\frac{1}{4}\bigl|\|x_{1}^{\omega}-z^{\omega}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}-\|x_{2}^{\omega}-z^{\omega}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}\bigr|\leq\frac{1}{4}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}.

If
x_{1}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}
and
x_{2}\in\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\omega}
, then

\displaystyle|f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\epsilon+\frac{1}{4}\|x_{1}^{\omega}-z^{\omega}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r^{\beta_{\omega}}}{4}\frac{\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}}{r^{\beta_{\omega}}}+\frac{1}{4}\|x_{1}^{\omega}-x_{2}^{\omega}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}.

If
x_{1}\in\bigcup_{j\in[d]:\omega_{j}=1}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
and
x_{2}\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\cup\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\omega}
, then
|f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|\leq 1/2\leq 1/2\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}
, and the result follows.
∎

Corollary S11
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>0
,
\beta_{\omega}\in(0,1]
,
\epsilon\in(0,(1/4)\wedge(1/8\cdot(r/q)^{\beta_{\omega}})]
,
\sigma=(\sigma_{t})_{t\in[T]}\in\{-1,1\}^{T}
. There exists a function
f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}:[0,\infty)^{d}\rightarrow\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}
such that for all
x\in\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\cup\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{S}^{\omega}\bigcup_{j\in[d]:\omega_{j}=1}\mathcal{R}_{j}^{\omega}
we have
f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)=f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)
; and further we have
f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})=f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1}^{\omega})
, as well as
|f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{1})-f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x_{2})|\leq(1/2)\cdot\lVert x_{1}-x_{2}\rVert^{\beta_{\omega}}
for all
x_{1},x_{2}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
.

Note that (
S6
) depends only on
x^{\omega}
, such that by McShane’s extension theorem
(
McShane, 1934
)
on
\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d_{\omega}}
there exists a
\beta_{\omega}
-Hölder continuous extension of
f^{\circ,+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x^{\omega})
onto
([0,\infty)^{d})^{\omega}
. The result follows by setting
f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)=f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x^{\omega})
for any
x\in[0,\infty)^{d}
.
∎

We now define the function
f_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}^{\omega}:\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}\rightarrow\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}
. Given
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{d})^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
s=(s_{1},\ldots,s_{d})^{T}\in\{-1,1\}^{d}
be
s_{j}:=\mathrm{sign}(x_{j})
and define

\displaystyle f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x):=\Bigl(\prod_{j\in[d_{\omega}]}\{\Pi_{\omega}(s)\}_{j}\Bigr)\cdot f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s\odot x).

(S7)

Finally, define
\eta_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}^{(\omega)}(x):=1/2+f_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}^{(\omega)}(x)
, for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Lemma S12
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\{0,1\}^{d}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>0
,
a,b\in(0,1/4]
, and
\bm{\beta}\in(0,1]^{d}
, and let
\beta_{\omega}:=\min\{\beta_{j}:\omega_{j}=1\}
. Fix further
\epsilon\in(0,(1/4)\wedge(1/8\cdot(r/q)^{\beta_{\omega}})]
,
\sigma=(\sigma_{t})_{t\in[T]}\in\{-1,1\}^{T}
and
c_{\mathrm{E}}\leq b/4
. Let
P=P^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma,a,b}
denote the distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}
with regression function
\eta=\eta^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
and marginal feature distribution
\mu=\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
. Further, let
Q=P\otimes U(\mathcal{O})
be the distribution of
(X,Y,O)
with
(X,Y)
-marginal
P
, with
O
distributed uniformly on
\mathcal{O}
and
(X,Y)\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}O
. Then
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{\omega\},c_{\mathrm{E}},\mathcal{O})
and
P\equiv P_{Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\bm{\beta},1)
.

First, since
O\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\models$}(X,Y)
, we have
\mu_{\omega^{\prime}}\equiv\mu_{\omega^{\prime}|o}
for any
o\in\mathcal{O}
and
\omega^{\prime}\preceq o
, thus
\sigma_{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}=\mbox{$\mathbb{E}$}_{Q}(f_{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}(X))
, for
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}
. Next, we show that the functions given by Equations (
4
)
and (
5
)
satisfy
f_{\omega}=f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
and
f_{\omega^{\prime}}\equiv 0
, for all
\omega^{\prime}\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\omega\}
.
By the symmetry of
f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
, and letting
j^{\star}=\min\{j\in[d]:~\omega_{j}=1\}
we have

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{0}_{d}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\bigl\{\eta(X)\bigr\}-\frac{1}{2}=\mathbb{E}_{P}\{f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(X)\}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{j^{\star}}>0\}}\,d\mu(x)+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{\{x_{j^{\star}}<0\}}\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{d-d_{\omega}}}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}:s_{j^{\star}}=1}\Bigl(\prod_{j=2}^{d_{\omega}}\{\Pi_{\omega}(s)\}_{j}\Bigr)\cdot\int_{\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\omega}}f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)\,d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\hskip 22.76219pt-\frac{1}{2^{d-d_{\omega}}}\sum_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}:s_{j^{\star}}=-1}\Bigl(\prod_{j=2}^{d_{\omega}}\{\Pi_{\omega}(s)\}_{j}\Bigr)\cdot\int_{\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\cup\mathcal{R}^{\omega}}f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)\,d\mu(x)=0,

where the fourth equality holds since
f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x)=0
wherever
x_{j^{\star}}=0
. Further, for
\mathbf{0}\prec\omega^{\prime}\prec\omega
, now letting
j^{\star}=\min\{j\in[d]:~\omega_{j}=1-\omega^{\prime}_{j}=1\}
and by induction we see that

\displaystyle f_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{\eta(X)-\frac{1}{2}-\sum_{\omega^{\prime\prime}\prec\omega^{\prime}}f_{\omega^{\prime\prime}}(X)\Big|X^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}}\Bigr\}=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(X)\Big|X^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(X)\bigl(\mathbbm{1}\{x_{j^{\star}}>0\}+\mathbbm{1}\{x_{j^{\star}}<0\}\bigr)\Big|X^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}}\Bigr\}=0

using the same argument as for the
\omega^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}_{d}
case. We further have

\displaystyle f_{\omega}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{\eta(X)-\frac{1}{2}-\sum_{\omega^{\prime}\prec\omega}f_{\omega^{\prime}}(X)\Big|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(X)\Big|X^{\omega}=x^{\omega}\Bigr\}=f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(x^{\omega}),

i.e.
f_{\omega}=f^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
. For
\omega^{\prime}\npreceq\omega
, there exists either a
j^{\star}=\min\{j\in[d]:~\omega_{j}=1-\omega^{\prime}_{j}=1\}
, in which case we proceed as above, or there exists no such
j^{\star}
, in which case
\omega\prec\omega^{\prime}
. In this case we have

\displaystyle f_{\omega^{\prime}}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{\eta(X)-\sum_{\omega^{\prime\prime}\prec\omega^{\prime}}f_{\omega^{\prime\prime}}(X)\Bigm|X^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}}\Bigr\}=\mathbb{E}_{P}\Bigl\{0\Bigm|X^{\omega^{\prime}}=x^{\omega^{\prime}}\Bigr\}=0.

We deduce that
\sigma_{\omega^{\prime}}^{2}=0
for all
\omega^{\prime}\neq\omega
. Finally, we have

\sigma_{\omega}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f_{\omega}^{2}(x)\,d\mu(x)=a\epsilon^{2}+\frac{b}{4}+2^{d_{\omega}}\int_{\mathcal{R}^{\omega}}\Bigl(\epsilon+\frac{1}{4}\|x^{\omega}-z^{\omega}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}\Bigr)^{2}\,d\mu(x)\geq b/4.

Thus
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{E}}(\{\omega\},c_{\mathrm{E}},\mathcal{O})
, for
c_{\mathrm{E}}\leq b/4
.

For the final part of the result, if
x_{1},x_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
are such that
\mathrm{sign}(x_{1}^{\omega})=\mathrm{sign}(x_{2}^{\omega})
, then by Corollary
S11
,

|f_{\omega}(x_{1})-f_{\omega}(x_{2})|=|f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s\odot x_{1})-f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s\odot x_{2})|\leq\frac{1}{2}\|s\odot x_{1}-s\odot x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}\leq\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}.

On the other hand, if
s_{1}:=\mathrm{sign}(x_{1}^{\omega})\neq\mathrm{sign}(x_{2}^{\omega})=:s_{2}
, then there exists
z\in\mathcal{S}^{\omega}
with
z=x_{1}+\zeta(x_{2}-x_{1})
for some
\zeta\in[0,1]
. Then, again by Corollary
S11
,

\displaystyle|f_{\omega}(x_{1})-

\displaystyle f_{\omega}(x_{2})|\leq|f_{\omega}(x_{1})-f_{\omega}(z)|+|f_{\omega}(z)-f_{\omega}(x_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq|f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s_{1}\odot x_{1})-f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s_{1}\odot z)|+|f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s_{2}\odot z)-f^{+}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}(s_{2}\odot x_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|s_{1}\odot x_{1}-s_{1}\odot z\|^{\beta_{\omega}}+\frac{1}{2}\|s_{2}\odot z-s_{2}\odot x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}=\frac{1}{2}\|x_{1}-z\|^{\beta_{\omega}}+\frac{1}{2}\|z-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\zeta^{\beta_{\omega}}+(1-\zeta)^{\beta_{\omega}})\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}\leq\frac{1}{2}2^{1-\beta_{\omega}}\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}\leq\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega}}.

Finally, for
\omega^{\prime}\neq\omega
we have
|f_{\omega^{\prime}}(x_{1})-f_{\omega^{\prime}}(x_{2})|=0\leq\|x_{1}-x_{2}\|^{\beta_{\omega^{\prime}}}
; In other words, we have
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{S}}(\bm{\beta},1)
.
∎

Lemma S13
.

Fix
d\in\mbox{$\mathbb{N}$}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
\kappa\in(0,1/\sqrt{d_{\omega}})
,
q\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>0
,
\beta_{\omega}\in(0,1]
,
\epsilon\in(0,(1/4)\wedge(1/8\cdot(r/q)^{\beta_{\omega}})]
,
\sigma=(\sigma_{t})_{t\in[T]}\in\{-1,1\}^{T}
.
Fix
C_{\mathrm{M}}\geq\max\{1+4^{d_{\omega}/\beta_{\omega}}(2\kappa)^{-d_{\omega}}V_{d_{\omega}},2^{\alpha}\}
,
\alpha\in[0,d_{\omega}/\beta_{\omega}]
, and
a\in[0,\epsilon^{\alpha}]
,
b\in(0,1/4]
. Let
P=P^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma,a,b}
denote the distribution on
\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\{0,1\}
with regression function
\eta=\eta^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
and marginal feature distribution
\mu=\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
. Then
P\in\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{M}}(\alpha,C_{\mathrm{M}})
.

First, if
t\in(0,\epsilon)
, then
\mu\bigl(\bigl\{x\in\mbox{$\mathbb{R}$}^{d}:|\eta(x)-1/2|<t\bigr\}\bigr)=0\leq C_{\mathrm{M}}\cdot t^{\alpha}
. For
t\in[\epsilon,1/2)
, by (
S6
) and the definition of
\eta
, if
x\in\mathrm{supp}(\mu)\setminus\bigcup_{s\in\{-1,1\}^{d}}(s\odot\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r})
satisfies
|\eta(x)-1/2|<t
, then
\|x-z^{\omega}\|\leq(4t)^{1/\beta_{\omega}}
. Thus

\displaystyle\mu\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\bigl|\eta(x)-1/2\bigr|<t\Bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=2^{d_{\omega}}\mu\bigl(\mathcal{T}^{\omega}_{q,r}\bigl)+2^{d_{\omega}}\mu\bigl(B_{(4t)^{1/\beta_{\omega}}}(z^{\omega})\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\bigr)

\displaystyle=a+\frac{(1-a-b)}{\kappa^{d_{\omega}}}\mathcal{L}_{d_{\omega}}\Bigl(\Pi_{\omega}\bigl(B_{(4t)^{1/\beta_{\omega}}}(z^{\omega})\cap\mathcal{R}^{\omega}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\alpha}+\frac{(1-a-b)(4t)^{d_{\omega}/\beta_{\omega}}V_{d_{\omega}}}{(2\kappa)^{d_{\omega}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{M}}\cdot t^{\alpha}.

Finally, if
t\geq 1/2
, then

\displaystyle\mu\Bigl(\Bigl\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\bigl|\eta(x)-1/2\bigr|<t\Bigr\}\Bigr)\leq 1\leq(2t)^{\alpha}\leq C_{\mathrm{M}}\cdot t^{\alpha},

as required.
∎

S2.4
Proof of the lower bound in the light tailed case

We can now complete the proof in the case that
\Omega_{\star}=\{\omega\}
is a singleton for which
\gamma_{\omega}=\min\{\gamma_{j}:\omega_{j}=1\}\geq 1
. Recall that
n_{\omega}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\omega\preceq o_{i}\}}
.

Lemma S14
.

Fix
d,n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\{0,1\}^{d}
,
o_{1},\ldots,o_{n}\in\mathcal{O}
,
\omega\in\{0,1\}^{d}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}_{d}\}
,
c_{\mathrm{E}}\in[0,1/4]
,
\bm{\gamma}\in[0,\infty)^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{L}}\geq 1
,
\bm{\beta}\in(0,1]^{d}
,
C_{\mathrm{S}}\geq 1
,
\alpha\in[0,\infty)
and
C_{\mathrm{M}}\geq 1
. Let
\beta_{\omega}:=\min\{\beta_{j}:\omega_{j}=1\}
. Suppose that
\gamma_{\omega}=\min\{\gamma_{j}:\omega_{j}=1\}\geq 1
,
\alpha\beta_{\omega}\leq d_{\omega}
,
C_{\mathrm{M}}\geq\max\{1+4^{d_{\omega}/\beta_{\omega}}(d_{\omega})^{-d_{\omega}/2}V_{d_{\omega}},2^{\alpha}\}
,
c_{\mathrm{E}}\leq 1/16
and
C_{\mathrm{L}}\geq 4^{\gamma_{\max}(d_{\omega}+1)}
, where
\gamma_{\max}=\max_{j\in[d]}\{\gamma_{j}\}
.
Then there exists a constant
c
, depending only on
\gamma_{\omega}
,
d_{\omega}
,
\beta_{\omega}
and
\alpha
, such that

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{C}\in\mathcal{C}_{n}}\sup_{Q}\mathbb{E}_{Q}\Bigl\{\mathcal{E}_{P_{Q}}(\hat{C})\Bigm|O_{1}=o_{1},\ldots,O_{n}=o_{n}\Bigr\}\geq c\cdot\max\Bigl\{n_{\omega}^{-\frac{\beta_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}(1+\alpha)}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}},1\Bigr\},

where the supremum is taken over
Q\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}^{\prime}(\{\omega\},c_{\mathrm{E}},\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\bm{\beta},C_{\mathrm{S}},\alpha,C_{\mathrm{M}},\mathcal{O})
.

First, let

a_{0}:=2^{-3d_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}}
,
a_{1}:=2^{3\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}/\beta_{\omega}+2\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}}\cdot d_{\omega}^{\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}/2}
,
b_{0}=2^{-1}(d_{\omega}2^{d_{\omega}})^{-\gamma_{\max}}
,
and

q_{0}:=\min\biggl\{a_{0}^{(2+\alpha)/(\alpha\wedge 1)}2^{5+d_{\omega}},\Bigr(\frac{a_{0}}{a_{1}}\cdot 2^{(d_{\omega}+5)\frac{\alpha\beta_{\omega}(1-\gamma_{\omega})+\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}}{\beta_{\omega}(2+\alpha)}}\Bigl)^{\frac{(2+\alpha)\beta_{\omega}}{\alpha\beta_{\omega}+\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}+2\gamma_{\omega}\beta_{\omega}}},2^{\frac{d_{\omega}(d_{\omega}-3\alpha-1)}{d_{\omega}+(2+\alpha)\beta_{\omega}}}\biggr\}.

Further let

\displaystyle\rho

\displaystyle:=\frac{\gamma_{\omega}(d_{\omega}-\alpha\beta_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}\in[0,1);\quad q:=\lfloor(q_{0}n_{\omega}^{\rho})^{1/d_{\omega}}\rfloor,

\displaystyle m

\displaystyle:=q^{d_{\omega}},\quad\epsilon:=\min\Bigl\{\Bigl(\frac{m}{2^{d_{\omega}+5}n_{\omega}}\Bigr)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}},1/4\Bigr\},\quad u:=\frac{\epsilon^{\alpha}}{m},

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{d_{\omega}}},\quad a:=\epsilon^{\alpha},\quad b:=\frac{1}{4},\quad r:=(8\epsilon)^{1/\beta_{\omega}}\cdot q.

Suppose initially that
n_{\omega}>q_{0}^{-1/\rho}
, so that
q\geq 1
. For
\sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{m}
, let
Q^{\sigma}
denote the distribution with marginal
\mu=\mu^{(\omega)}_{\kappa,r,q,a,b}
, regression function
\eta^{\sigma}=\eta^{(\omega)}_{\epsilon,q,r,\sigma}
and for which
O
is independent of
(X,Y)
and uniformly distributed on
\mathcal{O}
.

We now show that
Q^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}^{\prime}(\{\omega\},c_{\mathrm{E}},\bm{\gamma},C_{\mathrm{L}},\bm{\beta},C_{\mathrm{S}},\alpha,C_{\mathrm{M}},\mathcal{O})
, by applying in turn the results from the previous subsections. First,
Q^{\sigma}\in\mathcal{Q}_{\mathrm{Miss}}(\mathcal{O})
since the missingness indicator
O
is independent of
(X,Y)
and
\mbox{$\mathbb{P}$}(O=o)>0
for all
o\in\mathcal{O}
. Next, to apply Corollary
S9
, using that
\rho-1=\frac{-\gamma_{\omega}\beta_{\omega}(2+\alpha)}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta_{\omega}+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}
, we have

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=\epsilon^{\alpha}\leq\Bigl(\frac{m}{2^{d_{\omega}+5}n_{\omega}}\Bigr)^{\frac{\alpha}{2+\alpha}}\leq\Bigl(\frac{q_{0}n_{\omega}^{\rho}}{2^{d_{\omega}+5}n_{\omega}}\Bigr)^{\frac{\alpha}{2+\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\frac{q_{0}}{2^{5+d_{\omega}}}\Bigr)^{\alpha/(2+\alpha)}n_{\omega}^{-\frac{\gamma_{\omega}\alpha\beta_{\omega}}{\gamma_{\omega}(2\beta+d_{\omega})+\alpha\beta_{\omega}}}\leq a_{0}^{\alpha\wedge 1}\leq a_{0},

by the first term in the minimum in the definition of
q_{0}
. Further, this also implies that
\bigl(\frac{m}{2^{d_{\omega}+5}n_{\omega}}\bigr)^{\frac{1}{2+\alpha}}<\frac{1}{4}
, so
\epsilon<1/4
. Moreover, since
\alpha\beta_{\omega}\leq d_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle a^{1-\gamma_{\omega}}(4r\sqrt{d_{\omega}})^{\gamma_{\omega}d_{\omega}}
